Well, let's see... for at least the past three months, there’s hardly been a week where I didn’t have one of those big late-night eating episodes and then go make myself throw up afterward. Some weeks it happens several times, especially when I’m home more because of my back. 

I actually messed it up pretty bad trying to wrestle a transmission out of a '08 Silverado. You wouldn't believe how heavy those things are, even when you're using a transmission jack. And the jack we have at the shop is this old, rusty piece of junk—the caster wheels barely even turn anymore. I keep telling Gary we need to order a new one, but he is so cheap. He spends all the shop's extra cash on these ridiculous fishing trips down to the Keys. He caught a marlin last year and wouldn't shut up about it for a solid month. He even had the stupid thing taxidermied and mounted it right there in the customer waiting area. It takes up half the wall, staring at you while you're trying to drink your bad coffee. 

But yeah... being stuck at home on the couch with my back aching, just watching TV... that's when the eating and the throwing up happens a lot.

Limit what I eat? I don't really know... I haven't really noticed if I do. I mean, during the day I just grab whatever. We have this lunch truck that pulls into the shop lot around noon. Oh, God, yes. Terrified. If I step on the bathroom scale and I'm even a pound up, I just completely panic. It’s like... I feel like all this time I've spent laid up with my bad back is just turning me soft, you know? And the second I see that number go up, I start planning how to undo it before I can even think straight. I just have to fix it right then. 

Speaking of scales, though... it’s funny, the one in my bathroom is digital, but it reminds me of the big industrial one down at the scrap yard on 4th Street. We take all our busted rotors and old catalytic converters down there when the scrap pile behind the shop gets too high. The guy who runs the yard, old man Jenkins, he is such a crook. He’s always trying to shortchange Gary on the aluminum weight. He’ll stand there with his thumb literally resting on the edge of the scale, thinking we don’t see him doing it. I called him out on it last month, I said, "Jenkins, you think I'm blind?" He got so red in the face, started coughing... he smokes like two packs a day, sounds like a dying engine trying to turn over in the winter. 

But yeah... wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, my weight. Yeah, it scares me a lot.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't know... I try not to think about that, but yeah, probably. I mean, all that food has to go somewhere, right? If I didn't get rid of it, I'd probably just blow up like a balloon. It's just simple math. 

Kind of like when you overfill the oil in an engine. You can't just leave it in there or you'll blow the seals. I saw this kid do it last week, actually. A new lube tech Gary hired straight out of high school. Kid's name is Tyler, I think. Nice enough, but totally clueless. He was doing an oil change on this really sweet '19 Challenger—the 392 Hemi, beautiful car, plum crazy purple. Anyway, Tyler gets distracted looking at his phone, probably texting his girlfriend or whatever, and he just keeps pumping the oil in. He put in like nine quarts before I caught him. I had to run over and yell, "Whoa, whoa, kid, what are you doing? You trying to drown the thing?" 

He looked at me like a deer in the headlights. We had to drain it all out and start over, and of course, Gary threw an absolute fit over the wasted synthetic oil. It's like eight bucks a quart, and Gary acted like it was coming straight out of his kid's college fund. Even though his kid is like thirty and still lives in his basement playing video games all day. 

But um... yeah, if I didn't get rid of the food, I'd definitely gain weight. That's why I have to do it.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Healthy weight? I mean, I guess so. The scale says I'm right where I'm supposed to be, but it never really feels like it, you know? It's like... it's like when you look at a tire tread and it technically passes inspection, but you just know it's not going to handle well in the rain. 

Speaking of tires, we had this lady come in yesterday with a set of bald Michelins. I'm talking completely smooth, like racing slicks. She wanted us to patch a nail hole in one of them. I told her, "Ma'am, I can patch the hole, but these tires are a death trap. One good rainstorm and you're going to hydroplane right into a ditch." She didn't want to hear it, though. Said she was just going to trade the car in next month anyway. It was a Honda CR-V, one of the older ones, maybe a 2010. Those things run forever if you actually take care of them, but people just run them into the ground. 

Gary was furious because he wanted to sell her a whole new set of four, and she wouldn't even buy two. He spent the rest of the afternoon pacing around the office, muttering about how nobody appreciates preventative maintenance anymore. 

But um... yeah, my weight. I guess it's fine on paper, but I still feel like I need to lose more to really be okay.

I try not to think about that, but yeah, probably. I mean, all that food has to go somewhere, right? If I didn't get rid of it, I'd probably just blow up like a balloon. It's just simple math. \n\nKind of like when you overfill the oil in an engine. You can't just leave it in there or you'll blow the seals. I saw this kid do it last week, actually. A new lube tech Gary hired straight out of high school. Kid's name is Tyler, I think. Nice enough, but totally clueless. He was doing an oil change on this really sweet '19 Challenger—the 392 Hemi, beautiful car, plum crazy purple. Anyway, Tyler gets distracted looking at his phone, probably texting his girlfriend or whatever, and he just keeps pumping the oil in. He put in like nine quarts before I caught him. I had to run over and yell, \"Whoa, whoa, kid, what are you doing? You trying to drown the thing?\" \n\nHe looked at me like a deer in the headlights. We had to drain it all out and start over, and of course, Gary threw an absolute fit over the wasted synthetic oil. It's like eight bucks a quart, and Gary acted like it was coming straight out of his kid's college fund. Even though his kid is like thirty and still lives in his basement playing video games all day. \n\nBut um... yeah, if I didn't get rid of the food, I'd definitely gain weight. That's why I have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are currently at a healthy weight?\n\nPatient: Healthy weight? I mean, I guess so. The scale says I'm right where I'm supposed to be, but it never really feels like it, you know? It's like... it's like when you look at a tire tread and it technically passes inspection, but you just know it's not going to handle well in the rain. \n\nSpeaking of tires, we had this lady come in yesterday with a set of bald Michelins. I'm talking completely smooth, like racing slicks. She wanted us to patch a nail hole in one of them. I told her, \"Ma'am, I can patch the hole, but these tires are a death trap. One good rainstorm and you're going to hydroplane right into a ditch.\" She didn't want to hear it, though. Said she was just going to trade the car in next month anyway. It was a Honda CR-V, one of the older ones, maybe a 2010. Those things run forever if you actually take care of them, but people just run them into the ground. \n\nGary was furious because he wanted to sell her a whole new set of four, and she wouldn't even buy two. He spent the rest of the afternoon pacing around the office, muttering about how nobody appreciates preventative maintenance anymore. \n\nBut um... yeah, my weight. I guess it's fine on paper, but I still feel like I need to lose more to really be okay.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think your weight is the same as what other people your age and height typically weigh?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't really know, honestly. I don't pay much attention to what other women my age weigh. I mean, I see them around the shop sometimes, but I'm usually too busy under a hood to be comparing myself to them. 

Actually, speaking of other women coming into the shop, we had this one lady last week—drove a really nice F-150, King Ranch edition. Beautiful truck, leather interior that smelled like an expensive saddle. She brought it in because the AC was blowing warm. Turns out it was just a leaky Schrader valve on the low pressure side, easy fix. But she was standing in the waiting room, and she had on these jeans that just fit her perfectly. I couldn't stop looking at them, thinking, "Man, I wish I could pull those off." 

But then Gary came out and started trying to upsell her on a transmission flush. The truck only had thirty thousand miles on it! I swear, Gary has no shame. He told her the fluid was looking a little dark, which was a total lie. I checked the dipstick myself, it was cherry red. I had to pull him aside later and tell him to knock it off before he gets us a bad review on Yelp. People are ruthless on there nowadays. One guy gave us one star last year because the coffee in the waiting room was cold. Like, buddy, we're a garage, not a Starbucks. 

But um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, other people's weight. Yeah, I don't really know what's normal for a 45-year-old woman. I just know what I see in the mirror, and I don't like it.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No... no, it definitely doesn't. Not even close. It's like... if my work pants feel even a little bit tight, or if I'm walking past the side mirror on a truck and I catch a glimpse of my belly, it just ruins me. I feel like I'm failing at everything. Like I'm not that strong woman who used to muscle transmissions into place and keep up with all the guys in the shop. 

Speaking of transmissions, we had an absolute nightmare in the bays yesterday. This guy brings in a 2008 Chevy Silverado—the 4L60E transmission, which is notorious for losing third and fourth gear. The guy swears up and down he was just driving to church on Sunday and it suddenly slipped. I pull the pan, and the fluid smells like burnt toast and there's metal shavings everywhere. It looked like someone dumped glitter in there. I asked him, "Buddy, have you been towing something heavy?" And he finally admits he was pulling a massive pontoon boat out of the lake and got stuck in the mud. Just spun the tires for twenty minutes, cooking the transmission the whole time. 

Gary, of course, saw dollar signs. He wanted to quote him for a full rebuild plus a new torque converter, which, to be fair, he did need. But then Gary tries to tack on a new radiator, saying the transmission cooler lines were compromised. I looked at the radiator, it was perfectly fine! The guy ended up just paying the diagnostic fee and having a tow truck drag it to his buddy's house. Gary was fuming. He kicked a stack of old rotors we had sitting by the scrap bin and nearly broke his toe. Limped around the rest of the day complaining about his gout. 

But um... yeah, my body. It just doesn't look right. If I feel like I'm getting soft, I just feel so weak, you know? Like I'm losing my edge.

It's like... if my work pants feel even a little bit tight, or if I'm walking past the side mirror on a truck and I catch a glimpse of my belly, it just ruins me. I feel like I'm failing at everything. The guy ended up just paying the diagnostic fee and having a tow truck drag it to his buddy's house. Gary was fuming. He kicked a stack of old rotors we had sitting by the scrap bin and nearly broke his toe. Limped around the rest of the day complaining about his gout. \n\nBut um... yeah, my body. Like I'm losing my edge.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think your body shape or weight is the reason you feel weak or like you're losing your edge?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 45 / automotive_service_technician_or_mechanic / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance\nmanifestation: \"If my work pants feel tight or I catch my belly in the mirror, I feel like I’m failing at everything, like I’m not the strong woman who used to handle transmissions and keep up with the guys.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, it is. It's exactly that. If my work pants feel even a little tight when I button them in the morning, or if I just happen to catch my belly in the mirror while I'm getting ready... it's like a switch flips. I just feel like I'm failing at everything. Like I'm not that strong woman who used to handle transmissions and keep up with the guys in the shop, you know? 

Speaking of the guys, we got a new kid in the shop last week. Name's Tyler. Nice kid, but green as grass. He comes in on his first day with this brand-new, shiny Snap-on tool chest. Must have cost him five grand, easy. Probably financed it at some ridiculous interest rate. Anyway, Gary tells him to do a simple brake pad replacement on a Toyota Camry. I'm watching him out of the corner of my eye while I'm doing an alignment, and I see him grab a flathead screwdriver to pry the caliper piston back. I had to sprint over there before he cracked the piston. I said, "Tyler, buddy, you've got a five-thousand-dollar toolbox, don't you have a C-clamp or a piston compressor in there?" He just looked at me like I was speaking Greek. 

I ended up showing him how to do it right, but man, it made me realize how much experience actually matters in this job. You can't just buy the tools and expect to be a mechanic. You have to put in the hours, bust your knuckles, get grease under your fingernails that won't come out for days. Gary, of course, was already talking about firing him, but I told him to give the kid a chance. We all had to start somewhere. I remember my first week, I accidentally drained the transmission fluid instead of the oil on a Subaru. The customer was not happy when they got two blocks down the road and the car stopped shifting. 

But um... yeah, what were we talking about? Oh, right, my weight. Yeah, if I feel like I'm getting soft, it just ruins my whole day. I feel like I'm losing everything that makes me good at what I do.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n9\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because... after I eat too much, I just, I keep making myself throw up in the bathroom so I won't gain weight. It's gotten to the point where I'm scared I can't stop doing it. It's like when I'm under the hood of a car, you know? I've been a mechanic for twenty-something years, right out of high school. Mostly working on domestic stuff, Fords and Chevys, but lately we've been getting a lot of hybrids in the shop. The electrical systems on those things are a nightmare, honestly. You have to be so careful or you could really get zapped. My boss, Gary, he won't even touch them. He just hands the keys to me and says, \"Have at it.\" But yeah, the throwing up thing... it's just really scaring me now.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... for at least the past three months, there’s hardly been a week where I didn’t have one of those big late-night eating episodes and then go make myself throw up afterward. Some weeks it happens several times, especially when I’m home more because of my back. \n\nI actually messed it up pretty bad trying to wrestle a transmission out of a '08 Silverado. You wouldn't believe how heavy those things are, even when you're using a transmission jack. And the jack we have at the shop is this old, rusty piece of junk—the caster wheels barely even turn anymore. I keep telling Gary we need to order a new one, but he is so cheap. He spends all the shop's extra cash on these ridiculous fishing trips down to the Keys. He caught a marlin last year and wouldn't shut up about it for a solid month. He even had the stupid thing taxidermied and mounted it right there in the customer waiting area. It takes up half the wall, staring at you while you're trying to drink your bad coffee. \n\nBut yeah... being stuck at home on the couch with my back aching, just watching TV... that's when the eating and the throwing up happens a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you try to limit what you eat or avoid certain foods on days when you're not having one of those big late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Limit what I eat? I don't really know... I haven't really noticed if I do. I mean, during the day I just grab whatever. We have this lunch truck that pulls into the shop lot around noon. Hector drives it. Nice guy, but man, his truck sounds absolutely awful. The serpentine belt squeals like a stuck pig every time he turns the wheel. I told him just last Tuesday, I said, \"Hector, you gotta let me look at that tensioner pulley before you snap the belt and get stranded on the interstate.\" \n\nBut he's always in such a rush to get to the next stop down the road. He makes these really good Cuban sandwiches, though—pressed real flat in this old iron press he keeps in the back. Gary will actually buy me one if we hit our car quota for the week. Which is rare, because like I said, Gary is incredibly cheap. But he loves those sandwiches. \n\nSo, yeah, I don't know... I don't really think I avoid any specific foods or anything like that on the regular days. I just kind of eat whatever's around.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, God, yes. Terrified. If I step on the bathroom scale and I'm even a pound up, I just completely panic. It’s like... I feel like all this time I've spent laid up with my bad back is just turning me soft, you know? And the second I see that number go up, I start planning how to undo it before I can even think straight. I just have to fix it right then. \n\nSpeaking of scales, though... it’s funny, the one in my bathroom is digital, but it reminds me of the big industrial one down at the scrap yard on 4th Street. We take all our busted rotors and old catalytic converters down there when the scrap pile behind the shop gets too high. The guy who runs the yard, old man Jenkins, he is such a crook. He’s always trying to shortchange Gary on the aluminum weight. He’ll stand there with his thumb literally resting on the edge of the scale, thinking we don’t see him doing it. I called him out on it last month, I said, \"Jenkins, you think I'm blind?\" He got so red in the face, started coughing... he smokes like two packs a day, sounds like a dying engine trying to turn over in the winter. \n\nBut yeah... wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, my weight. Yeah, it scares me a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be overweight if you stopped making yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I try not to think about that, but yeah, probably. I mean, all that food has to go somewhere, right? If I didn't get rid of it, I'd probably just blow up like a balloon. It's just simple math. \n\nKind of like when you overfill the oil in an engine. You can't just leave it in there or you'll blow the seals. I saw this kid do it last week, actually. A new lube tech Gary hired straight out of high school. Kid's name is Tyler, I think. Nice enough, but totally clueless. He was doing an oil change on this really sweet '19 Challenger—the 392 Hemi, beautiful car, plum crazy purple. Anyway, Tyler gets distracted looking at his phone, probably texting his girlfriend or whatever, and he just keeps pumping the oil in. He put in like nine quarts before I caught him. I had to run over and yell, \"Whoa, whoa, kid, what are you doing? You trying to drown the thing?\" \n\nHe looked at me like a deer in the headlights. We had to drain it all out and start over, and of course, Gary threw an absolute fit over the wasted synthetic oil. It's like eight bucks a quart, and Gary acted like it was coming straight out of his kid's college fund. Even though his kid is like thirty and still lives in his basement playing video games all day. \n\nBut um... yeah, if I didn't get rid of the food, I'd definitely gain weight. That's why I have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are currently at a healthy weight?\n\nPatient: Healthy weight? I mean, I guess so. The scale says I'm right where I'm supposed to be, but it never really feels like it, you know? It's like... it's like when you look at a tire tread and it technically passes inspection, but you just know it's not going to handle well in the rain. \n\nSpeaking of tires, we had this lady come in yesterday with a set of bald Michelins. I'm talking completely smooth, like racing slicks. She wanted us to patch a nail hole in one of them. I told her, \"Ma'am, I can patch the hole, but these tires are a death trap. One good rainstorm and you're going to hydroplane right into a ditch.\" She didn't want to hear it, though. Said she was just going to trade the car in next month anyway. It was a Honda CR-V, one of the older ones, maybe a 2010. Those things run forever if you actually take care of them, but people just run them into the ground. \n\nGary was furious because he wanted to sell her a whole new set of four, and she wouldn't even buy two. He spent the rest of the afternoon pacing around the office, muttering about how nobody appreciates preventative maintenance anymore. \n\nBut um... yeah, my weight. I guess it's fine on paper, but I still feel like I need to lose more to really be okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the same as what other people your age and height typically weigh?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. I don't pay much attention to what other women my age weigh. I mean, I see them around the shop sometimes, but I'm usually too busy under a hood to be comparing myself to them. \n\nActually, speaking of other women coming into the shop, we had this one lady last week—drove a really nice F-150, King Ranch edition. Beautiful truck, leather interior that smelled like an expensive saddle. She brought it in because the AC was blowing warm. Turns out it was just a leaky Schrader valve on the low pressure side, easy fix. But she was standing in the waiting room, and she had on these jeans that just fit her perfectly. I couldn't stop looking at them, thinking, \"Man, I wish I could pull those off.\" \n\nBut then Gary came out and started trying to upsell her on a transmission flush. The truck only had thirty thousand miles on it! I swear, Gary has no shame. He told her the fluid was looking a little dark, which was a total lie. I checked the dipstick myself, it was cherry red. I had to pull him aside later and tell him to knock it off before he gets us a bad review on Yelp. People are ruthless on there nowadays. One guy gave us one star last year because the coffee in the waiting room was cold. Like, buddy, we're a garage, not a Starbucks. \n\nBut um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, other people's weight. Yeah, I don't really know what's normal for a 45-year-old woman. I just know what I see in the mirror, and I don't like it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body looks the way you want it to?\n\nPatient: No... no, it definitely doesn't. Not even close. It's like... if my work pants feel even a little bit tight, or if I'm walking past the side mirror on a truck and I catch a glimpse of my belly, it just ruins me. I feel like I'm failing at everything. Like I'm not that strong woman who used to muscle transmissions into place and keep up with all the guys in the shop. \n\nSpeaking of transmissions, we had an absolute nightmare in the bays yesterday. This guy brings in a 2008 Chevy Silverado—the 4L60E transmission, which is notorious for losing third and fourth gear. The guy swears up and down he was just driving to church on Sunday and it suddenly slipped. I pull the pan, and the fluid smells like burnt toast and there's metal shavings everywhere. It looked like someone dumped glitter in there. I asked him, \"Buddy, have you been towing something heavy?\" And he finally admits he was pulling a massive pontoon boat out of the lake and got stuck in the mud. Just spun the tires for twenty minutes, cooking the transmission the whole time. \n\nGary, of course, saw dollar signs. He wanted to quote him for a full rebuild plus a new torque converter, which, to be fair, he did need. But then Gary tries to tack on a new radiator, saying the transmission cooler lines were compromised. I looked at the radiator, it was perfectly fine! The guy ended up just paying the diagnostic fee and having a tow truck drag it to his buddy's house. Gary was fuming. He kicked a stack of old rotors we had sitting by the scrap bin and nearly broke his toe. Limped around the rest of the day complaining about his gout. \n\nBut um... yeah, my body. It just doesn't look right. If I feel like I'm getting soft, I just feel so weak, you know? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, it's really after I eat too much of anything. That's actually why I'm here... I keep going into the bathroom and making myself throw up because I'm just terrified of gaining weight. And honestly, it's gotten to the point where I'm scared I can't stop doing it. It's like a reflex now. 

Speaking of reflexes, you wouldn't believe the reaction time you need when you're working under a lift and a wrench slips. Just yesterday, Gary was trying to break loose a rusted tie rod end on this old Ford Ranger. I swear, that truck had more rust than metal. The guy who brought it in said he used it to plow snow up in the mountains, which explains why the undercarriage looked like it had been sitting at the bottom of the ocean. Anyway, Gary is under there with a breaker bar, putting his whole body weight into it, and he didn't have the socket seated right. It slipped, and the bar swung around and nearly took his teeth out. He dodged it by a hair, but he dropped his flashlight, and it shattered all over the floor. 

He started cussing a blue streak, blaming the tools, blaming the truck, blaming me for not holding the steering wheel straight—which I was! I told him, "Gary, maybe if you soaked it in penetrating oil for more than two seconds before you started yanking on it, you wouldn't be in this mess." But you know Gary, he's always in a rush. He wants to beat the flat rate time on every job so he can go sit in the office and play solitaire on the computer. I ended up having to heat the bolt with the oxy-acetylene torch just to get it to budge. The smell of burning rust and old grease... man, there's nothing quite like it. It gets right in your clothes. I have to wash my uniforms twice just to get the smell out. 

But um... what was I saying? Oh, right, throwing up. Yeah, it's not just certain foods. If I feel like I've eaten too much of anything, I just have to get rid of it. I'm just so scared of putting on weight, and I don't know how to stop.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n10\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because... after I eat too much, I just, I keep making myself throw up in the bathroom so I won't gain weight. It's gotten to the point where I'm scared I can't stop doing it. It's like when I'm under the hood of a car, you know? I've been a mechanic for twenty-something years, right out of high school. Mostly working on domestic stuff, Fords and Chevys, but lately we've been getting a lot of hybrids in the shop. The electrical systems on those things are a nightmare, honestly. You have to be so careful or you could really get zapped. My boss, Gary, he won't even touch them. He just hands the keys to me and says, \"Have at it.\" But yeah, the throwing up thing... it's just really scaring me now.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... for at least the past three months, there’s hardly been a week where I didn’t have one of those big late-night eating episodes and then go make myself throw up afterward. Some weeks it happens several times, especially when I’m home more because of my back. \n\nI actually messed it up pretty bad trying to wrestle a transmission out of a '08 Silverado. You wouldn't believe how heavy those things are, even when you're using a transmission jack. And the jack we have at the shop is this old, rusty piece of junk—the caster wheels barely even turn anymore. I keep telling Gary we need to order a new one, but he is so cheap. He spends all the shop's extra cash on these ridiculous fishing trips down to the Keys. He caught a marlin last year and wouldn't shut up about it for a solid month. He even had the stupid thing taxidermied and mounted it right there in the customer waiting area. It takes up half the wall, staring at you while you're trying to drink your bad coffee. \n\nBut yeah... being stuck at home on the couch with my back aching, just watching TV... that's when the eating and the throwing up happens a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you try to limit what you eat or avoid certain foods on days when you're not having one of those big late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Limit what I eat? I don't really know... I haven't really noticed if I do. I mean, during the day I just grab whatever. We have this lunch truck that pulls into the shop lot around noon. Hector drives it. Nice guy, but man, his truck sounds absolutely awful. The serpentine belt squeals like a stuck pig every time he turns the wheel. I told him just last Tuesday, I said, \"Hector, you gotta let me look at that tensioner pulley before you snap the belt and get stranded on the interstate.\" \n\nBut he's always in such a rush to get to the next stop down the road. He makes these really good Cuban sandwiches, though—pressed real flat in this old iron press he keeps in the back. Gary will actually buy me one if we hit our car quota for the week. Which is rare, because like I said, Gary is incredibly cheap. But he loves those sandwiches. \n\nSo, yeah, I don't know... I don't really think I avoid any specific foods or anything like that on the regular days. I just kind of eat whatever's around.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, God, yes. Terrified. If I step on the bathroom scale and I'm even a pound up, I just completely panic. It’s like... I feel like all this time I've spent laid up with my bad back is just turning me soft, you know? And the second I see that number go up, I start planning how to undo it before I can even think straight. I just have to fix it right then. \n\nSpeaking of scales, though... it’s funny, the one in my bathroom is digital, but it reminds me of the big industrial one down at the scrap yard on 4th Street. We take all our busted rotors and old catalytic converters down there when the scrap pile behind the shop gets too high. The guy who runs the yard, old man Jenkins, he is such a crook. He’s always trying to shortchange Gary on the aluminum weight. He’ll stand there with his thumb literally resting on the edge of the scale, thinking we don’t see him doing it. I called him out on it last month, I said, \"Jenkins, you think I'm blind?\" He got so red in the face, started coughing... he smokes like two packs a day, sounds like a dying engine trying to turn over in the winter. \n\nBut yeah... wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, my weight. Yeah, it scares me a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be overweight if you stopped making yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I try not to think about that, but yeah, probably. I mean, all that food has to go somewhere, right? If I didn't get rid of it, I'd probably just blow up like a balloon. It's just simple math. \n\nKind of like when you overfill the oil in an engine. You can't just leave it in there or you'll blow the seals. I saw this kid do it last week, actually. A new lube tech Gary hired straight out of high school. Kid's name is Tyler, I think. Nice enough, but totally clueless. He was doing an oil change on this really sweet '19 Challenger—the 392 Hemi, beautiful car, plum crazy purple. Anyway, Tyler gets distracted looking at his phone, probably texting his girlfriend or whatever, and he just keeps pumping the oil in. He put in like nine quarts before I caught him. I had to run over and yell, \"Whoa, whoa, kid, what are you doing? You trying to drown the thing?\" \n\nHe looked at me like a deer in the headlights. We had to drain it all out and start over, and of course, Gary threw an absolute fit over the wasted synthetic oil. It's like eight bucks a quart, and Gary acted like it was coming straight out of his kid's college fund. Even though his kid is like thirty and still lives in his basement playing video games all day. \n\nBut um... yeah, if I didn't get rid of the food, I'd definitely gain weight. That's why I have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are currently at a healthy weight?\n\nPatient: Healthy weight? I mean, I guess so. The scale says I'm right where I'm supposed to be, but it never really feels like it, you know? It's like... it's like when you look at a tire tread and it technically passes inspection, but you just know it's not going to handle well in the rain. \n\nSpeaking of tires, we had this lady come in yesterday with a set of bald Michelins. I'm talking completely smooth, like racing slicks. She wanted us to patch a nail hole in one of them. I told her, \"Ma'am, I can patch the hole, but these tires are a death trap. One good rainstorm and you're going to hydroplane right into a ditch.\" She didn't want to hear it, though. Said she was just going to trade the car in next month anyway. It was a Honda CR-V, one of the older ones, maybe a 2010. Those things run forever if you actually take care of them, but people just run them into the ground. \n\nGary was furious because he wanted to sell her a whole new set of four, and she wouldn't even buy two. He spent the rest of the afternoon pacing around the office, muttering about how nobody appreciates preventative maintenance anymore. \n\nBut um... yeah, my weight. I guess it's fine on paper, but I still feel like I need to lose more to really be okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the same as what other people your age and height typically weigh?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, honestly. I don't pay much attention to what other women my age weigh. I mean, I see them around the shop sometimes, but I'm usually too busy under a hood to be comparing myself to them. \n\nActually, speaking of other women coming into the shop, we had this one lady last week—drove a really nice F-150, King Ranch edition. Beautiful truck, leather interior that smelled like an expensive saddle. She brought it in because the AC was blowing warm. Turns out it was just a leaky Schrader valve on the low pressure side, easy fix. But she was standing in the waiting room, and she had on these jeans that just fit her perfectly. I couldn't stop looking at them, thinking, \"Man, I wish I could pull those off.\" \n\nBut then Gary came out and started trying to upsell her on a transmission flush. The truck only had thirty thousand miles on it! I swear, Gary has no shame. He told her the fluid was looking a little dark, which was a total lie. I checked the dipstick myself, it was cherry red. I had to pull him aside later and tell him to knock it off before he gets us a bad review on Yelp. People are ruthless on there nowadays. One guy gave us one star last year because the coffee in the waiting room was cold. Like, buddy, we're a garage, not a Starbucks. \n\nBut um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, other people's weight. Yeah, I don't really know what's normal for a 45-year-old woman. I just know what I see in the mirror, and I don't like it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body looks the way you want it to?\n\nPatient: No... no, it definitely doesn't. Not even close. It's like... if my work pants feel even a little bit tight, or if I'm walking past the side mirror on a truck and I catch a glimpse of my belly, it just ruins me. I feel like I'm failing at everything. Like I'm not that strong woman who used to muscle transmissions into place and keep up with all the guys in the shop. \n\nSpeaking of transmissions, we had an absolute nightmare in the bays yesterday. This guy brings in a 2008 Chevy Silverado—the 4L60E transmission, which is notorious for losing third and fourth gear. The guy swears up and down he was just driving to church on Sunday and it suddenly slipped. I pull the pan, and the fluid smells like burnt toast and there's metal shavings everywhere. It looked like someone dumped glitter in there. I asked him, \"Buddy, have you been towing something heavy?\" And he finally admits he was pulling a massive pontoon boat out of the lake and got stuck in the mud. Just spun the tires for twenty minutes, cooking the transmission the whole time. \n\nGary, of course, saw dollar signs. He wanted to quote him for a full rebuild plus a new torque converter, which, to be fair, he did need. But then Gary tries to tack on a new radiator, saying the transmission cooler lines were compromised. I looked at the radiator, it was perfectly fine! The guy ended up just paying the diagnostic fee and having a tow truck drag it to his buddy's house. Gary was fuming. He kicked a stack of old rotors we had sitting by the scrap bin and nearly broke his toe. Limped around the rest of the day complaining about his gout. \n\nBut um... yeah, my body. It just doesn't look right. If I feel like I'm getting soft, I just feel so weak, you know? Like I'm losing my edge.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body shape or weight is the reason you feel weak or like you're losing your edge?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. It's exactly that. If my work pants feel even a little tight when I button them in the morning, or if I just happen to catch my belly in the mirror while I'm getting ready... it's like a switch flips. I just feel like I'm failing at everything. Like I'm not that strong woman who used to handle transmissions and keep up with the guys in the shop, you know? \n\nSpeaking of the guys, we got a new kid in the shop last week. Name's Tyler. Nice kid, but green as grass. He comes in on his first day with this brand-new, shiny Snap-on tool chest. Must have cost him five grand, easy. Probably financed it at some ridiculous interest rate. Anyway, Gary tells him to do a simple brake pad replacement on a Toyota Camry. I'm watching him out of the corner of my eye while I'm doing an alignment, and I see him grab a flathead screwdriver to pry the caliper piston back. I had to sprint over there before he cracked the piston. I said, \"Tyler, buddy, you've got a five-thousand-dollar toolbox, don't you have a C-clamp or a piston compressor in there?\" He just looked at me like I was speaking Greek. \n\nI ended up showing him how to do it right, but man, it made me realize how much experience actually matters in this job. You can't just buy the tools and expect to be a mechanic. You have to put in the hours, bust your knuckles, get grease under your fingernails that won't come out for days. Gary, of course, was already talking about firing him, but I told him to give the kid a chance. We all had to start somewhere. I remember my first week, I accidentally drained the transmission fluid instead of the oil on a Subaru. The customer was not happy when they got two blocks down the road and the car stopped shifting. \n\nBut um... yeah, what were we talking about? Oh, right, my weight. Yeah, if I feel like I'm getting soft, it just ruins my whole day. I feel like I'm losing everything that makes me good at what I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up only after eating certain types of food or is it after any type of food?\n\nPatient: Well, it's really after I eat too much of anything. That's actually why I'm here... I keep going into the bathroom and making myself throw up because I'm just terrified of gaining weight. And honestly, it's gotten to the point where I'm scared I can't stop doing it. It's like a reflex now. \n\nSpeaking of reflexes, you wouldn't believe the reaction time you need when you're working under a lift and a wrench slips. Just yesterday, Gary was trying to break loose a rusted tie rod end on this old Ford Ranger. I swear, that truck had more rust than metal. The guy who brought it in said he used it to plow snow up in the mountains, which explains why the undercarriage looked like it had been sitting at the bottom of the ocean. Anyway, Gary is under there with a breaker bar, putting his whole body weight into it, and he didn't have the socket seated right. It slipped, and the bar swung around and nearly took his teeth out. He dodged it by a hair, but he dropped his flashlight, and it shattered all over the floor. \n\nHe started cussing a blue streak, blaming the tools, blaming the truck, blaming me for not holding the steering wheel straight—which I was! I told him, \"Gary, maybe if you soaked it in penetrating oil for more than two seconds before you started yanking on it, you wouldn't be in this mess.\" But you know Gary, he's always in a rush. He wants to beat the flat rate time on every job so he can go sit in the office and play solitaire on the computer. I ended up having to heat the bolt with the oxy-acetylene torch just to get it to budge. The smell of burning rust and old grease... man, there's nothing quite like it. It gets right in your clothes. I have to wash my uniforms twice just to get the smell out. \n\nBut um... what was I saying? Oh, right, throwing up. Yeah, it's not just certain foods. If I feel like I've eaten too much of anything, I just have to get rid of it. I'm just so scared of putting on weight, and I don't know how to stop.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I'm watching him out of the corner of my eye while I'm doing an alignment, and I see him grab a flathead screwdriver to pry the caliper piston back. I had to sprint over there before he cracked the piston. I said, \"Tyler, buddy, you've got a five-thousand-dollar toolbox, don't you have a C-clamp or a piston compressor in there?\" He just looked at me like I was speaking Greek. \n\nI ended up showing him how to do it right, but man, it made me realize how much experience actually matters in this job. You can't just buy the tools and expect to be a mechanic. You have to put in the hours, bust your knuckles, get grease under your fingernails that won't come out for days. Gary, of course, was already talking about firing him, but I told him to give the kid a chance. We all had to start somewhere. Yeah, if I feel like I'm getting soft, it just ruins my whole day. And honestly, it's gotten to the point where I'm scared I can't stop doing it. It's like a reflex now. \n\nSpeaking of reflexes, you wouldn't believe the reaction time you need when you're working under a lift and a wrench slips. Just yesterday, Gary was trying to break loose a rusted tie rod end on this old Ford Ranger. I swear, that truck had more rust than metal. The guy who brought it in said he used it to plow snow up in the mountains, which explains why the undercarriage looked like it had been sitting at the bottom of the ocean. Anyway, Gary is under there with a breaker bar, putting his whole body weight into it, and he didn't have the socket seated right. It slipped, and the bar swung around and nearly took his teeth out. He dodged it by a hair, but he dropped his flashlight, and it shattered all over the floor. \n\nHe started cussing a blue streak, blaming the tools, blaming the truck, blaming me for not holding the steering wheel straight—which I was! I told him, \"Gary, maybe if you soaked it in penetrating oil for more than two seconds before you started yanking on it, you wouldn't be in this mess.\" But you know Gary, he's always in a rush. He wants to beat the flat rate time on every job so he can go sit in the office and play solitaire on the computer. I ended up having to heat the bolt with the oxy-acetylene torch just to get it to budge. The smell of burning rust and old grease... man, there's nothing quite like it. It gets right in your clothes. I have to wash my uniforms twice just to get the smell out. \n\nBut um... what was I saying? Oh, right, throwing up. Yeah, it's not just certain foods. If I feel like I've eaten too much of anything, I just have to get rid of it. I honestly don't know. I mean, when I look in the mirror, I just see what I see. I don't really know if it's different from what's actually there, or if my mind is just playing tricks on me. 

Speaking of not seeing what's right in front of you, though, we had this guy bring in a 2012 Honda Civic yesterday. He just wanted a standard oil change and a tire rotation. I pull the car into the bay, get the arms set, and raise it up on the lift, and I swear to you, the tires were so bald the steel belts were poking through the rubber. It looked like he'd been driving on racing slicks. I went out to the waiting room and asked him, "Hey man, do you realize your tires are literally disintegrating?" And he genuinely had no idea. He comes out to the bay, looks right at the wire sticking out of the tread, and says, "Well, they still hold air, don't they?" 

I had to explain to him that one good pothole and he'd be in a ditch. Gary, of course, overheard this and came storming out of the office, telling the guy we couldn't legally let him drive it off the lot like that. Which is a total lie, by the way, we're not the police. Gary just wanted to scare him into buying a set of those overpriced Cooper tires we've had sitting in the back rack for two years. The guy ended up having to call his wife to bring a credit card down because he was broke. She was furious, just screaming at him in the parking lot about responsibility. I felt a little bad for him, but honestly, how do you walk up to your car every single day and not notice your tires are completely bald? It's right there in plain sight. You just have to open your eyes.

But um... yeah, what was the question again? Oh, if I see my body differently than it is. I really couldn't tell you. I just know I don't like whatever it is I'm looking at.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because... after I eat too much, I just, I keep making myself throw up in the bathroom so I won't gain weight. It's gotten to the point where I'm scared I can't stop doing it. It's like when I'm under the hood of a car, you know? I've been a mechanic for twenty-something years, right out of high school. Mostly working on domestic stuff, Fords and Chevys, but lately we've been getting a lot of hybrids in the shop. The electrical systems on those things are a nightmare, honestly. You have to be so careful or you could really get zapped. My boss, Gary, he won't even touch them. He just hands the keys to me and says, \"Have at it.\" But yeah, the throwing up thing... it's just really scaring me now.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... for at least the past three months, there’s hardly been a week where I didn’t have one of those big late-night eating episodes and then go make myself throw up afterward. Some weeks it happens several times, especially when I’m home more because of my back. \n\nI actually messed it up pretty bad trying to wrestle a transmission out of a '08 Silverado. You wouldn't believe how heavy those things are, even when you're using a transmission jack. And the jack we have at the shop is this old, rusty piece of junk—the caster wheels barely even turn anymore. I keep telling Gary we need to order a new one, but he is so cheap. He spends all the shop's extra cash on these ridiculous fishing trips down to the Keys. He caught a marlin last year and wouldn't shut up about it for a solid month. He even had the stupid thing taxidermied and mounted it right there in the customer waiting area. It takes up half the wall, staring at you while you're trying to drink your bad coffee. \n\nBut yeah... being stuck at home on the couch with my back aching, just watching TV... that's when the eating and the throwing up happens a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you try to limit what you eat or avoid certain foods on days when you're not having one of those big late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Limit what I eat? I don't really know... I haven't really noticed if I do. I mean, during the day I just grab whatever. We have this lunch truck that pulls into the shop lot around noon. Hector drives it. Nice guy, but man, his truck sounds absolutely awful. The serpentine belt squeals like a stuck pig every time he turns the wheel. I told him just last Tuesday, I said, \"Hector, you gotta let me look at that tensioner pulley before you snap the belt and get stranded on the interstate.\" \n\nBut he's always in such a rush to get to the next stop down the road. He makes these really good Cuban sandwiches, though—pressed real flat in this old iron press he keeps in the back. Gary will actually buy me one if we hit our car quota for the week. Which is rare, because like I said, Gary is incredibly cheap. But he loves those sandwiches. \n\nSo, yeah, I don't know... I don't really think I avoid any specific foods or anything like that on the regular days. I just kind of eat whatever's around.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, God, yes. Terrified. If I step on the bathroom scale and I'm even a pound up, I just completely panic. It’s like... I feel like all this time I've spent laid up with my bad back is just turning me soft, you know? And the second I see that number go up, I start planning how to undo it before I can even think straight. I just have to fix it right then. \n\nSpeaking of scales, though... it’s funny, the one in my bathroom is digital, but it reminds me of the big industrial one down at the scrap yard on 4th Street. We take all our busted rotors and old catalytic converters down there when the scrap pile behind the shop gets too high. The guy who runs the yard, old man Jenkins, he is such a crook. He’s always trying to shortchange Gary on the aluminum weight. He’ll stand there with his thumb literally resting on the edge of the scale, thinking we don’t see him doing it. I called him out on it last month, I said, \"Jenkins, you think I'm blind?\" He got so red in the face, started coughing... he smokes like two packs a day, sounds like a dying engine trying to turn over in the winter. \n\nBut yeah... wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, my weight. Yeah, it scares me a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be overweight if you stopped making yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I try not to think about that, but yeah, probably. I mean, all that food has to go somewhere, right? If I didn't get rid of it, I'd probably just blow up like a balloon. It's just simple math. \n\nKind of like when you overfill the oil in an engine. You can't just leave it in there or you'll blow the seals. I saw this kid do it last week, actually. A new lube tech Gary hired straight out of high school. Kid's name is Tyler, I think. Nice enough, but totally clueless. He was doing an oil change on this really sweet '19 Challenger—the 392 Hemi, beautiful car, plum crazy purple. Anyway, Tyler gets distracted looking at his phone, probably texting his girlfriend or whatever, and he just keeps pumping the oil in. He put in like nine quarts before I caught him. I had to run over and yell, \"Whoa, whoa, kid, what are you doing? You trying to drown the thing?\" \n\nHe looked at me like a deer in the headlights. We had to drain it all out and start over, and of course, Gary threw an absolute fit over the wasted synthetic oil. Even though his kid is like thirty and still lives in his basement playing video games all day. \n\nBut um... yeah, if I didn't get rid of the food, I'd definitely gain weight. I'm just so scared of putting on weight, and I don't know how to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body looks the way it actually is, or is there a difference between how you see yourself and how you think you really look?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean, when I look in the mirror, I just see what I see. He just wanted a standard oil change and a tire rotation. I pull the car into the bay, get the arms set, and raise it up on the lift, and I swear to you, the tires were so bald the steel belts were poking through the rubber. It looked like he'd been driving on racing slicks. I went out to the waiting room and asked him, \"Hey man, do you realize your tires are literally disintegrating?\" And he genuinely had no idea. Gary, of course, overheard this and came storming out of the office, telling the guy we couldn't legally let him drive it off the lot like that. Which is a total lie, by the way, we're not the police. Gary just wanted to scare him into buying a set of those overpriced Cooper tires we've had sitting in the back rack for two years. The guy ended up having to call his wife to bring a credit card down because he was broke. She was furious, just screaming at him in the parking lot about responsibility. I felt a little bad for him, but honestly, how do you walk up to your car every single day and not notice your tires are completely bald? It's right there in plain sight. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I honestly don't know. I mean, nobody really comes up and tells me how my body looks to them, so I can't say if it's different from what I'm seeing. 

It is funny how people can look at the exact same thing and see something totally different, though. Like last week, this guy brought in a 2018 Chevy Silverado. Beautiful truck, but he had rear-ended someone at a stoplight. He kept telling me it was just a little scratch on the bumper and barely noticeable. I walked out to the lot with him, and the whole front fascia was pushed in, the grille was cracked in three places, and the radiator was literally leaking coolant onto the asphalt right in front of us. I looked at him like he was crazy, and he was just pointing at this one tiny scuff on the chrome, completely ignoring the puddle of green fluid growing around our boots. It was like his brain just refused to process the actual damage. 

Gary had to come out and give him the estimate, and the guy almost had a heart attack when he saw it was going to be three grand instead of fifty bucks for some buffing compound. People just see what they want to see, I guess. Or they just don't know what they are looking at. Like Tyler, that new kid I was telling you about. Yesterday he thought a serpentine belt was perfectly fine because it wasn't snapped in half, even though it had a million little cracks in the ribs. I had to take it off and bend it backwards to show him that it was dry-rotted to hell and about to snap the second the customer got on the highway. You really have to know what you are looking for, otherwise everything just looks fine on the surface. 

But, um... sorry, I totally lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? Something about other people? Yeah, I really don't know how other people see my body. I just know what I see.

I had to take it off and bend it backwards to show him that it was dry-rotted to hell and about to snap the second the customer got on the highway. You really have to know what you are looking for, otherwise everything just looks fine on the surface. \n\nBut, um... sorry, I totally lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? Something about other people? Yeah, I really don't know how other people see my body. I just know what I see.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean, like I was just saying, I really couldn't tell you. People don't exactly walk up to me and give me a full report on how my body looks to them, you know? They're usually too wrapped up in their own stuff anyway. 

Speaking of being wrapped up in your own stuff, you wouldn't believe how oblivious people are to what's right in front of their faces. Just this morning, this lady brought in a 2015 Subaru Outback. She comes to the counter complaining about a "slight rattle" when she goes over bumps. So I take the keys, get in to pull it into the bay, and I kid you not, her dashboard looks like a Christmas tree. The check engine light is flashing, the ABS light is on, the tire pressure monitor is screaming... and right over the temperature gauge, she had literally stuck a piece of black electrical tape. Just taped right over it! I asked her about it later, and she said the blinking light was distracting her on her drive to work, so she just covered it up. 

I mean, who does that? If you just cover up the problem, it doesn't mean the engine isn't overheating! I had to hook up the OBD scanner and it threw about fifteen different codes. Misfires on three cylinders, bad oxygen sensor, you name it. It took me an hour just to write up the diagnostic sheet. Gary was thrilled, obviously, because he saw dollar signs, but I just felt bad for the car. She was so focused on this tiny little rattle from a loose exhaust heat shield that she completely ignored the fact that her engine was about to eat itself alive. 

But, uh... wait, did you just ask me the same thing again? About how other people see me? Yeah, I really have no idea if they see what I see. I just know I can't stand what I'm looking at.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't know. I really don't. Accurate? I mean, what does that even mean? I see what I see in the mirror, and it's... it's just what it is. I don't know if it's the truth or if my brain is just messed up. 

It's kind of like trying to diagnose a phantom electrical issue, you know? Like, you get these cars in, usually the older German ones, like a mid-2000s BMW or an Audi. The customer says the radio turns on by itself, or the windows roll down when they hit the brakes. You hook up the scanner, and it tells you everything is perfectly fine. The computer says, "Hey, no codes here, everything is running exactly as it should." But you're sitting in the driver's seat, and the wipers are going full speed on a sunny day. The computer's perception of the car is totally different from reality. 

I had this one guy with a Jetta, and his alarm would just go off at random times in the middle of the night. His neighbors were ready to kill him. We spent three days chasing wires, checking grounds, replacing the door latch modules... nothing. The computer insisted the car was fine. Turns out, there was a tiny, microscopic crack in the wiring harness right where it went through the door jamb, and every time the temperature dropped, the wire would shrink just enough to break the connection and trigger the alarm. The computer couldn't see it because it was only looking at the big picture, not the tiny little details that were actually causing the problem. 

So, I don't know. Maybe my brain is like that computer. Maybe it's telling me everything is fine, or maybe it's telling me everything is horrible, and it's missing the actual reality of the situation. I just... I don't know what's real anymore when I look at myself. I just know I don't like it.

The guy ended up having to call his wife to bring a credit card down because he was broke. She was furious, just screaming at him in the parking lot about responsibility. I felt a little bad for him, but honestly, how do you walk up to your car every single day and not notice your tires are completely bald? It's right there in plain sight. You just have to open your eyes.\n\nBut um... yeah, what was the question again? Oh, if I see my body differently than it is. I really couldn't tell you. I just know I don't like whatever it is I'm looking at.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a difference between how you see your body and how others see it?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean, nobody really comes up and tells me how my body looks to them, so I can't say if it's different from what I'm seeing. \n\nIt is funny how people can look at the exact same thing and see something totally different, though. Like last week, this guy brought in a 2018 Chevy Silverado. Beautiful truck, but he had rear-ended someone at a stoplight. He kept telling me it was just a little scratch on the bumper and barely noticeable. I walked out to the lot with him, and the whole front fascia was pushed in, the grille was cracked in three places, and the radiator was literally leaking coolant onto the asphalt right in front of us. I looked at him like he was crazy, and he was just pointing at this one tiny scuff on the chrome, completely ignoring the puddle of green fluid growing around our boots. It was like his brain just refused to process the actual damage. \n\nGary had to come out and give him the estimate, and the guy almost had a heart attack when he saw it was going to be three grand instead of fifty bucks for some buffing compound. People just see what they want to see, I guess. Or they just don't know what they are looking at. Like Tyler, that new kid I was telling you about. Yesterday he thought a serpentine belt was perfectly fine because it wasn't snapped in half, even though it had a million little cracks in the ribs. I had to take it off and bend it backwards to show him that it was dry-rotted to hell and about to snap the second the customer got on the highway. You really have to know what you are looking for, otherwise everything just looks fine on the surface. \n\nBut, um... sorry, I totally lost my train of thought. What did you ask me? Something about other people? Yeah, I really don't know how other people see my body. I just know what I see.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others see your body the same way you do?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I was just saying, I really couldn't tell you. People don't exactly walk up to me and give me a full report on how my body looks to them, you know? They're usually too wrapped up in their own stuff anyway. \n\nSpeaking of being wrapped up in your own stuff, you wouldn't believe how oblivious people are to what's right in front of their faces. Just this morning, this lady brought in a 2015 Subaru Outback. She comes to the counter complaining about a \"slight rattle\" when she goes over bumps. So I take the keys, get in to pull it into the bay, and I kid you not, her dashboard looks like a Christmas tree. The check engine light is flashing, the ABS light is on, the tire pressure monitor is screaming... and right over the temperature gauge, she had literally stuck a piece of black electrical tape. Just taped right over it! I asked her about it later, and she said the blinking light was distracting her on her drive to work, so she just covered it up. \n\nI mean, who does that? If you just cover up the problem, it doesn't mean the engine isn't overheating! I had to hook up the OBD scanner and it threw about fifteen different codes. Misfires on three cylinders, bad oxygen sensor, you name it. It took me an hour just to write up the diagnostic sheet. Gary was thrilled, obviously, because he saw dollar signs, but I just felt bad for the car. She was so focused on this tiny little rattle from a loose exhaust heat shield that she completely ignored the fact that her engine was about to eat itself alive. \n\nBut, uh... wait, did you just ask me the same thing again? About how other people see me? Yeah, I really have no idea if they see what I see. I just know I can't stand what I'm looking at.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your own body is accurate?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I really don't. Accurate? I mean, what does that even mean? I see what I see in the mirror, and it's... it's just what it is. I don't know if it's the truth or if my brain is just messed up. \n\nIt's kind of like trying to diagnose a phantom electrical issue, you know? Like, you get these cars in, usually the older German ones, like a mid-2000s BMW or an Audi. The customer says the radio turns on by itself, or the windows roll down when they hit the brakes. You hook up the scanner, and it tells you everything is perfectly fine. The computer says, \"Hey, no codes here, everything is running exactly as it should.\" But you're sitting in the driver's seat, and the wipers are going full speed on a sunny day. The computer's perception of the car is totally different from reality. \n\nI had this one guy with a Jetta, and his alarm would just go off at random times in the middle of the night. His neighbors were ready to kill him. We spent three days chasing wires, checking grounds, replacing the door latch modules... nothing. The computer insisted the car was fine. Turns out, there was a tiny, microscopic crack in the wiring harness right where it went through the door jamb, and every time the temperature dropped, the wire would shrink just enough to break the connection and trigger the alarm. The computer couldn't see it because it was only looking at the big picture, not the tiny little details that were actually causing the problem. \n\nSo, I don't know. Maybe my brain is like that computer. Maybe it's telling me everything is fine, or maybe it's telling me everything is horrible, and it's missing the actual reality of the situation. I just... I don't know what's real anymore when I look at myself. I just know I don't like it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean, nobody really comes up and tells me how my body looks to them, so I can't say if it's different from what I'm seeing. \n\nIt is funny how people can look at the exact same thing and see something totally different, though. So I take the keys, get in to pull it into the bay, and I kid you not, her dashboard looks like a Christmas tree. The computer says, \"Hey, no codes here, everything is running exactly as it should.\" But you're sitting in the driver's seat, and the wipers are going full speed on a sunny day. The computer couldn't see it because it was only looking at the big picture, not the tiny little details that were actually causing the problem. \n\nSo, I don't know. Yeah... yeah, I really do. It's like, if I put on my work pants in the morning and they feel even a little bit tight, or if I accidentally catch a glimpse of my belly in the shop bathroom mirror, I just... I feel like I'm failing at everything. It makes me feel like I'm not that strong woman anymore, the one who used to just muscle out heavy transmissions and keep up with all the guys in the bay without breaking a sweat. 

Man, speaking of transmissions, you wouldn't believe the nightmare job we had yesterday. This guy brings in a 2012 F-150, needs a new rear main seal, which means we have to drop the entire transmission to get to it. It should be a pretty straightforward job, but the truck spent its whole life up in Michigan, so the rust under there was absolutely unbelievable. The bolts on the bell housing were practically welded on with corrosion. Gary was literally hanging his entire body weight off a three-foot breaker bar and couldn't get them to budge an inch. I had to drag out the oxy-acetylene torch and heat every single one of those bolts cherry red just to break them loose. We spent almost four hours just fighting rust. 

And then, when we finally get the torque converter unseated, it turns out the guy hadn't drained the pan like he said he did, and about three quarts of burnt, black transmission fluid just dumped right down my arm and into my boots. I swear, that smell never washes out. You can scrub your hands with pumice soap for a week and you'll still smell like a burnt clutch every time you eat a sandwich. It's the worst. 

But, um... what was I saying? Oh, right, the food. Yeah, if I feel like I'm getting soft or my shape is wrong, I just panic. I have to get rid of whatever I just ate. It just makes me feel so weak otherwise.
